   Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview

   >From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
   >To: <post.nul>
   >Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 13:46:55 -0300
   >Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
   >>To: post.nul
   >>Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 10:00:23 +0100
   >>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>>From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
   >>>To: <post.nul>
   >>>Date: Thu, 29 Apr 2010 16:09:16 -0300
   >>>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>>I find it difficult to believe that after many thousands of years
   >>>these CTs were able to clean up after themselves and not leave a
   >>>trace of themselves anywhere.
   >>The only way this could happen is if they didn't originate here
   >>- in which case, of course, the CTH collapses back into a type
   >>of ETH.
   >>>And the other question is; why would they bother?
   >>Indeed.
   >>What I don't quite understand is why the CTH/ETH debate gets so
   >>heated. If there is a hypothesis about entities who are
   >>sufficiently advanced to make our technology look extremely
   >>primitive, then making an issue of their point of origin seems
   >>rather silly: the chances are they'll be galactic citizens with
   >>a long and complex migratory history.
   >I'm not sure where you got the idea this was heated, Gerald.
   I got the idea from listening to comments made in the interview
   to which this thread refers. And also from previous discussions
   on this List. It wasn't a reference to this specific thread.
   --
   Gerald O'Connell

                                                   Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   From: Jason Gammon <boyinthemachine.nul>
   Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 17:39:52 -0400
   Archived: Sat, 01 May 2010 07:45:02 -0400
   Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview

   >From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
   >To: <post.nul>
   >Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 12:14:02 -0700
   >Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
   >>To: <post.nul>
   >>Date: Thu, 29 Apr 2010 16:09:16 -0300
   >>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   <snip>
   >>It really doesn't matter about overburden, Ed. That won't hide
   >>much. And it will be longer that just awhile. Modern materials
   >>will last in to the tens of thousands of years. Gold contacts in
   >>electronic connections will last until the Sun goes supernova.
   >Yes all that is correct, but I'm talking about fifty to sixty
   >million years ago. And as I've said, I have reason to believe
   >that Cryptoterrestrial evolution took place in Antarctica during
   >an extended warming period.
   What evidence would that be? How convenient they lived in
   Antartica.
   <snip>
   >>>the origin and evolutionary history of Cryptoterrestrials was
   >>>never discussed. What I found refreshing was a discussion among
   >>>four Ufologists who have studied the issue very closely, and
   >>>have no doubts about visitation, but have rejected the ETH and
   >>Paul Kimball didn't reject it entirely.
   >>>are openly willing to consider the Cryptoterrestrial theory,
   >>>what ever that might end up being.
   >>I've considered it and rejected it.
   >I think you've ignored and/or disregarded and rejected the
   >information on which I base my case, namely the features of
   >creature in the AA footage, and the AA debris footage, the crash
   >sites I've found and examined, the cameraman's story, and the
   >sketches he submitted, and most of all the nature and meaning of
   >the cristobalite that was found covering the AA crash site.
   Why in the world are you bringing up the hoax that is Alien
   Autopsy?
   >You've not alone, of course, since 99.99 % of other Ufologists
   >agree and have chosen to side with the ETH even though
   >Cryptoterrestrial theory doesn't conflict with abductions, or
   >strange crafts in our sky, or big-headed, dark-eyed creatures.
   >Even without the information the Alien Autopsy provides, one
   >might think of the Cryptoterrestrial theory as a strong
   >possibility. Its only drawback, as you've pointed out, is lack
   >of evidence of a previous civilization. But you don't have any
   >evidence of life on other planets, or their ability to travel at


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m01-002.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:06]
Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview

   >light speed, and that doesn't seem to bother you. There's
   >nothing in Biology that prevents considering the evolution of
   >another mammal toward tool making, but there are numerous
   >obstacles to star travel found in a basic Physics text.
   <snip>
   >>>They probably started just as we did, by using natural products
   >>>and molding them to their needs. Once they mastered tool
   >>>making, then given enough time, they mastered other forms of
   >>>technology. Using H.sapiens as a template, we know that drastic
   >>>changes can easily occur over a few thousands of years.
   >>So what did they use?
   >The same basic materials we used: stone, wood, bone, shells, and
   >metal, once they discovered its qualities.
   I seriously doubt UFOs are composed of wood, bone, and shells.
   <snip>
   >They are our mammalian cousins. They've been here for at least
   >fifty million years. They are monotremes, to be exact.
   So now you are making the claim that the unproven cryptos were
   monotremes? Wow. I suppose you have a fossilized cypto egg
   stashed away somewhere?

   -Jason Gammon

                                                   Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
   Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 14:41:40 -0700
   Archived: Sat, 01 May 2010 08:02:48 -0400
   Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview

   >From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
   >To: <post.nul>
   >Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 15:38:26 -0400
   >Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
   >>To: <post.nul>
   >>Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 12:14:02 -0700
   >>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>But if you and the other researchers continue to ignore
   >>information gleaned from the AA and the crash site, then
   >>you'll continue to be dumbfounded by the UFO phenomenon.
   >Thank you, Ed. I couldn't have put it better myself.
   >I think you've just about said all that those curious about
   >cryptoterrestrials and their advocates need to know.
   Jerry,
   I certainly can't speak for other advocates of the
   Cryptoterrestrial theory. They have their reasons, and I have
   mine. What are your reasons for rejecting the Cryptoterrestrial
   theory, aside from my connecting it to the Alien Autopsy. Don't
   you think that another earth-based, tool making civilization is
   entirely possible?
   Have you ever watched the AA & debris footage in slow motion?
   Why do you think it's a hoax? Did someone tell you that it was
   or have you really studied this complex evidence and found it
   riddled with errors?
   Do you understand the facts regarding the Cristobalite debate I
   had with Ray Stanford and why it was so important? Do you
   understand why I think the creature in the AA is a monotreme?
   I didn't miss the sarcasm, but I don't really see the point to
   it. "Still, a man hears what he wants to hear And disregards the
   rest." I guess that could apply to both of us.

   Ed

                                                   Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   From: Paul Kimball <robieshark.nul>
   Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 21:42:05 -0300
   Archived: Sat, 01 May 2010 08:05:14 -0400
   Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview

   >From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
   >To: <post.nul>
   >Date: Thu, 29 Apr 2010 16:09:16 -0300
   >Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>From: Ed Gehrma <egehrman.nul>
   >>To: <post.nul>
   >>Date: Wed, 28 Apr 2010 13:49:46 -0700
   >>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
   >>the origin and evolutionary history of Cryptoterrestrials was
   >>never discussed. What I found refreshing was a discussion among
   >>four Ufologists who have studied the issue very closely, and
   >>have no doubts about visitation, but have rejected the ETH and
   >Paul Kimball didn't reject it entirely.
   Hi Don,
   Nothing like having my views misrepresented by Ed.
   You are quite correct. Not only do I not reject the ETH, I still
   consider it the most likely non-prosaic explanation for the UFO
   phenomenon. I am also far from a supporter of the CTH. Indeed,
   as I told Mac Tonnies when he was alive, I didn't think it was
   very likely. Something often missed in the discussion is that
   Mac didn't view it as some sort of established fact, either.
   What I reject is the intellectual rigidity of those people who
   accept the ETH as a proven fact, which I think is an
   unsustainable position.
   To me, the UFO phenomenon remains an interesting mystery. There
   are many possible answers - including the ETH. But there are no
   proven answers for those cases that remain unexplained.
   Hope that straightens things out.

   Paul

   >>Thank you, Ed. I couldn't have put it better myself.

   >>cryptoterrestrials and their advocates need to know.
   >I certainly can't speak for other advocates of the
   >Cryptoterrestrial theory. They have their reasons, and I have
   >mine. What are your reasons for rejecting the Cryptoterrestrial
   >theory, aside from my connecting it to the Alien Autopsy. Don't
   >you think that another earth-based, tool making civilization is
   >entirely possible?

   >I didn't miss the sarcasm, but I don't really see the point to
   >it. "Still, a man hears what he wants to hear And disregards the

   The problem, Ed, is not that I've heard what I want to hear but
   that, after five decades in this field, I've heard it all.
   Your pronouncement that those of us who will remain dumbfounded
   about UFOs if we don't heed your profound understanding calls to
   mind, for example, the sort of thing I used to hear from
   followers of George Adamski. So do the demands to throw all else
   aside and immerse myself in the study the "evidence", however
   manifestly dubious.
   Cryptoterrestrials are simply the latest iteration in lost-race
   mythology, which from Atlanteans and Lemurians to Shaver's
   cavern dwellers, and more. Much more, in fact... well, at least
   in romantic supposition. My next book, Hidden Realms, due out a
   month from today, examines some of these. It doesn't mention
   cryptoterrestrials, but the book I will be writing over the next
   several months definitely will.
   Of course you can believe whatever you want, but my own interest
   in the CT movement will remain purely clinical and folkloric,
   guided by the melancholy understanding that, in dreams as in
   life, some things never change.

   other critics should take the time to revisit the AA. The
   creature is real. I know this is hard to believe but if you want
   to begin to understand the true nature of UFO, the AA and the
   crash site is where to begin.

                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > May > May 1

                                        SETI [And UFOs] In The Wall Street Journal
  From: Rich Heiden <rwheidenu.nul>
  Date: Sat, 1 May 2010 08:17:40 -0700 (PDT)
  Archived: Sat, 01 May 2010 11:56:38 -0400
  Subject: SETI [And UFOs] In The Wall Street Journal

  Paul Davies in the Wall Street Journal, about the failure of
  SETI. One of the letters to the editor a week later (mine!)
  brings up the UFO angle.
  Wall Street Journal, April 10-11, 2010, pp. W1-W2.
  "Is Anybody Out There? After 50 years, astronomers haven't found
  any signs of intelligent life beyond Earth. They could be
  looking in the wrong places" by Paul Davies. The subtitle makes
  a lot of sense, but after that, it's all downhill.
  http://tinyurl.com/23ewxtg
  ---
  Wall Street Journal, April 17-18, 2010, p. A12, the 2nd of 4
  groups of letters in the Letters To The Editor column.
  http://tinyurl.com/2ezfchz
  ---
  My original letter as sent to the Wall Street Journal:
  Subject: Paul Davies in April 10-11 issue
  Tuesday, April 13, 2010 1:55 PM
  From: "Rich Heiden" <rwheiden*.nul>
  To: wsj.ltrs.nul
  To the Journal,
  I must respond to Paul Davies's article in the April 10-11
  Weekend Journal (pp. W1-W2), about the failure of the search for
  extraterrestrial intelligence. Not once does he mention UFOs,
  not even to put them down, or to argue that [all] they "have to
  be" something other than extraterrestrial. As the saying goes,
  "None is so blind as he who will not see."

  Richard W. Heiden
  Milwaukee

  From: Cathy Reason <CathyM.nul>
  Date: Sat, 1 May 2010 17:08:10 +0100

  Subject: Re: NASA Evidence Of Life On Mars

  >From: Nick Pope <nick.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Fri, 30 Apr 2010 11:23:44 +0200 (CEST)
  >Subject: Re: NASA Evidence Of Life On Mars
  >Although snipped out, my original post pointed out that The Sun
  >sourced their story from NASA's news conference.
  >Newspapers both inform and entertain. The Sun takes an
  >unashamedly populist stance and there's a lot of snobbery about
  >that.
  Actually there's very little snobbery about the Sun, though
  there is a considerable degree of victim stancing, whereby the
  Sun and its apologists claim to be the victims of snobbery.
  On the other hand there is a good deal of criticism of the Sun,
  not on account of its populist style, but on account of the
  extremely tenuous relationship which exists between the stories
  which appear in its pages, and the real-world events to which
  those stories notionally refer.
  >When I write for tabloids like The Sun or The Mirror, I write
  >in their house style. When I write for broadsheets like The
  >Times or The Guardian, I write in their house style:
  The house style being, presumably, that facts may be made to
  appear and disappear at will.

  Cathy

   >consider it the most likely non-prosaic explanation for the UFO
   >phenomenon. I am also far from a supporter of the CTH. Indeed,
   >as I told Mac Tonnies when he was alive, I didn't think it was
   >very likely. Something often missed in the discussion is that
   >Mac didn't view it as some sort of established fact, either.
   >What I reject is the intellectual rigidity of those people who
   >accept the ETH as a proven fact, which I think is an
   >unsustainable position.
   I have never accepted it as a proven fact, Paul, but I consider
   it a viable contender.
   What troubles me about the CTH is that there should be some
   evidence of it somewhere in the geological slash archeological
   record.
   If one of our own, Ray Stanford, can find 110 million year old
   fossils (the nodosaurus among others) and preserved baby dino
   prints in a stream bed near his home in Wash., DC (now on
   display in the Smithsonian Museum of Natural History - congrats
   Ray) why haven't we come across the record of some so-existing
   species?
   A dinosaur (pick one) leaves fossilized remnants of its bones
   and sometimes even impressions of skin or feathers and that's
   about it. You might even find parts of some creature that it was
   eating.
   Now fast forward to humans and their sites dating back 5.5
   million years and what do you find... garbage. Mind you it was
   primitive garbage back then. Early humans would likely have had


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m02-002.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:12]
Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview

   tribal wars over the sophisticated garbage of modern man. We are
   plagued with garbage and it is a multi-billion dollar industry
   often evidencing corruption on a large scale.
   Where is the Crypto garbage? Technology breeds garbage, even if
   it's just beads or stone tools. I asked in an earlier email if
   the Cryptos were so really well practiced that they always
   removed traces of themselves. And at what point did they decide
   to hide themselves and for what reason.
   >To me, the UFO phenomenon remains an interesting mystery. There
   >are many possible answers - including the ETH. But there are no
   >proven answers for those cases that remain unexplained.
   Would you care to expand on the other possible answers? Ican guess one.
   >Hope that straightens things out.
   Your position yes, but there is still a big puzzle to solve and
   it's not getting any easier, Paul.

   Don Ledger

New Book By Thomas E. Bullard




                                                         UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > May > May 2

                                                  New Book By Thomas E. Bullard
  From: Michael J Briggs <mbriggs.nul>
  Date: Sat, 1 May 2010 15:57:58 -0500
  Archived: Sun, 02 May 2010 04:32:01 -0400
  Subject: New Book By Thomas E. Bullard

  Ten years after publishing UFOs And Abductions (edited by David
  Jacobs), University Press of Kansas is hitting the UFO trail
  again, this time with Thomas "Eddie" Bullard's new book which
  we're publishing later this year.
  -----
  The Myth and Mystery of UFOs
  by
  Thomas E. Bullard
  University Press of Kansas
  www.kansaspress.ku.edu
  October 2010
  456 pages, 24 photographs, 61.8 x 91.4
  cloth ISBN 978-0-7006-1729-6,
  $34.95
  When United Airlines workers reported a UFO at O'Hare Airport in
  November 2006, it was met with the typical denials and hush-up
  that usually accompany such sightings. But when a related story
  broke the record for hits at the Chicago Tribune's website, it
  was clear that such unexplained objects continued to occupy the
  minds of fascinated readers. Why, wonders Thomas Bullard, don't
  such persistent sightings command more urgent attention from
  scientists, scholars, and mainstream journalists?
  The answer, in part, lies in Bullard's wide-ranging magisterial
  survey of the mysterious, frustrating, and ever-evolving
  phenomenon that refuses to go away and our collective efforts to
  understand it. In his trailblazing book, Bullard views those
  efforts through the lens of mythmaking, discovering what UFO
  accounts tell us about ourselves, our beliefs, and the
  possibility of visitors from beyond.
  Bullard shows how ongoing grassroots interest in UFOs stems both
  from actual personal experiences and from a cultural mythology
  that defines such encounters as somehow "alien"=97and how it views
  relentless official denial as a part of conspiracy to hide the
  truth. He also describes how UFOs have catalyzed the evolution
  of a new but highly fractured belief system that borrows heavily
  from the human past and mythic themes and which UFO witnesses
  and researchers use to make sense of such phenomena and our
  place in the cosmos.
  Bullard's book takes in the whole spectrum of speculations on
  alien visitations and abductions, magically advanced
  technologies, governmental conspiracies, varieties of religious
  salvation, apocalyptic fears, and other paranormal experiences.
  Along the way, Bullard investigates how UFOs have inspired
  books, movies, and television series; blurred the boundaries
  between science, science fiction, and religion; and crowded the
  Internet with websites and discussion groups. From the patches
  of this crazy quilt, he posits evidence that a genuine
  phenomenon seems to exist outside the myth.
  Enormously erudite and endlessly engaging, Bullard's study is a
  sky watcher's guide to the studies, stories, and debates that


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m02-006.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:14]
New Book By Thomas E. Bullard

  this elusive subject has inspired. It shows that, despite all
  the competing interests and errors clouding the subject, there
  is substance beneath the clutter, a genuinely mysterious
  phenomenon that deserves attention as more than a myth.
  Thomas E. Bullard is a board member of the Center for UFO
  Studies and Fund for UFO Research and author of UFO Abductions:
  The Measure of a Mystery and The Sympathetic Ear: Investigators
  as Variables in UFO Abduction Reports.
  ---
  "A wide-ranging and wonderfully informed look at an elusive and
  difficult subject. Bullard, a folklorist, does a remarkable job
  of analyzing the UFO-and-abduction phenomenon's similarities
  with ancient myths and legends." --David M. Jacobs, editor of
  UFOs And Abductions: Challenging The Borders Of Knowledge

  "A compelling and admirable study that combines lucidity and
  intellectual rigor. Bullard outlines in eloquent prose the
  difference between the stimulus (puzzling sightings and
  encounters) and the stimulated (witnesses, investigators,
  theorists, hoaxers, skeptics, and spiritual seekers) to reveal a
  complex and shifting phenomenon that continues to challenge our
  understanding of reality." --Jerome clark, author of The UFO
  Book and The UFO EncyclOpedia
  -----
  Best wishes,
  Mike
  Michael Briggs
  Editor-in-Chief
  University Press of Kansas
  www.kansaspress.ku.edu

  unless you've tapped into the major publishing house's marketing
  system.
  There are a couple of full-time UFO researchers who have
  marketed their skills and are able to provide for themselves and
  their families, but I don't think anyone's getting rich in this.
  The Fund has many UFO publications for sale, but those who want
  specific reports have purchased them and after a year or so the
  information becomes stale and less interesting to newcomers.
  In the end, the cost of providing these limited numbers is not
  profitable and we're working with other organizations to somehow
  make them available.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m02-009.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:15]
Re: No Money In Ufology



  To Joe McGonagle... Just tell us how you really feel about the AA
  'film'... <g>
  The possibility that Ray Santilli will be taken to Court for
  fraud is unlikely, as the cost of pursuing that legal action
  would exceed any monetary reward you might receive. The FOX
  Network is probably the most likely entity to file any kind of
  suit, and I've not heard that they were discussing that to any
  degree.
  There are a few who would like to produce another program on the
  making of the AA 'film' (at least from the evidence they've
  collected), but there's no demand for it and they can't sell the
  idea.
  As you are probably aware, there has been a side discussion that
  developed regarding the AA 'film' and these are participants who
  became involved when it first came out and are still debating
  aspects of it.
  This discussion was joined by Spyros Melaris, who Philip has
  written about and who claims to be the brains behind the hoax.
  Spyros plans on writing an expose' on the AA 'film' to once and
  for all get credit for a creation that he had developed with Ray
  Santilli's funding (from Volker Spielberg, I believe) and I
  hope it is completed soon.
  Ed and Philip were both among those in this small AA List and
  Ed would argue that Spyros has his own agenda and is lying, so
  I'm merely presenting this to full out the picture a bit.
  Personally, I believe that Spyros has provided enough detail to
  show that he was intimately involved in the production of two AA
  'film' segments, under contract with Ray Santilli.
  IMO, the Ant and Dec movie wasn't the best story to be told when
  you look at the entire tale. I hope it gets written.

  Steve

  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Sun, 2 May 2010 10:00:42 -0400

  Subject: Re: Alien Autopsy Nonsense

  >From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Sat, 01 May 2010 17:49:28 +0100
  >Subject: Alien Autopsy Nonsense [was: Cryptoterrestrial Interview]
  >>From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
  >>To: <post.nul>
  >>Date: Sat, 1 May 2010 07:56:35 -0700
  >>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
  <snip>
  >>Ray did not admit that the AA was a hoax. He stated that he and
  >>his friends "restored" some of the AA footage. He hasn't
  >>presented any evidence that this occurred. The AA history has
  >>been twisted and distorted by lies, deception and false
  >>testimony. But at its core, it is legitimate. There's not a
  >>single scrap of evidence that it is a hoax.
  >If this wasn't UFO-related, I would have been astounded at what
  >people are prepared to believe in spite of all of the known
  >deceit and manipulation involved in this film. As it stands, it
  >no longer surprises me what people will clutch on to within
  >ufology.
  I can understand your exasperation, Joe, but while you're right
  on the main point - that the AA film is an absurd fabrication -
  you're wrong on a couple of levels.
  First of all, ufologists are no different from anybody else - I
  could cite numerous examples just off the top of my head (but
  won't for fear of starting another kind of flame war) - in
  sometimes holding to dubious and discredited beliefs. Ufology,
  contrary to another kind of mythology promoted by another self-
  interested, self-validating subculture, is not some unique
  social pathology, as you seem to have it. It's just a group of
  human beings behaving like a group of human beings.
  Second, at least here in the U.S., the AA film made hardly
  impact on ufologists. Ed's is an island of gullibility in a sea
  of skepticism. Where the AA film had an impact - in other words,
  where it was taken seriously - was among the non-ufologist mass
  TV-viewing public. (I learned that from personal experience in
  the town where I live.) As a general principle, in American
  ufology the AA film hardly rose to the level of "controversy" .
  It was more like the level of head-shaking.

  Jerry Clark

  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>

  Subject: Re: No Money In Ufology

  >From: Jim Deardorff <deardorj.nul>

  >>To: <post.nul>
  >>Date: Sat, 1 May 2010 12:12:41 -0400
  >>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
  >>>From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
  >>>To: <post.nul>
  >>>Date: Sat, 1 May 2010 08:38:00 -0700
  >>>Subject: Re: Cryptoterrestrial Interview
  >>I don't write books or sell tickets or plan to make a single
  >>cent on any of my research....
  >>That's mighty virtuous of you, Ed, and just as meaningless. I
  >>can tell you that if you were to try, you'd learn that there's
  >>no money worth speaking of to be made in UFO writing anyway.
  >>Believe me, I wish that weren't true.
  >I'm glad you've come around to that viewpoint, Jerry.
  What a strange statement, not to mention a condescending one.
  I have been writing professionally about UFOs and anomalies
  since the 1970s. (My first book, co-written with Loren Coleman,
  was published in 1975.) If anybody knows about what kind of
  financial reward (or lack of same) one may expect from that
  career decision, I do. (Another world-class authority is Jenny
  Randles.) So, Jim, I didn't "come around to that viewpoint." I
  have known it, after long private struggle and on a deeply
  personal level, far longer than I suspect you have.
  >It's been
  >my observation also. But both within and outside of ufology the
  >money-making claim has often been used to put down undesired
  >cases.
  >For instance, at the end of your UFO Encyclopedia article on
  >Meier you quote from Korff that "The Billy Meier 'evidence' is
  >now a money-making machine."
  I think most - obviously not you - would agree that what Meier
  is doing has nothing to do with ufology except for superficial
  appearance. What it has to do with, I'm sure he alone knows with
  certainty.
  When I think of ufologists, I think of individuals who research
  the UFO phenomenon, not of persons who claim to cavort on a
  fairly regular basis with ETs. I agree that the latter's motives
  cannot always be easily pigeon-holed. For example, a conscious
  hoaxer who hoped to make money but didn't may stay with the
  story for other reasons, ones he may not have anticipated
  initially. He may even develop a certain measure of belief in
  his own claims. Many contactees, on the other hand, are sincere
  from the outset - though that doesn't obligate the rest of us to


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m02-012.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:17]
Re: No Money In Ufology

  take their stories as literal truth.
  The psychology of contactees is frequently complex. I have my
  own theories and impressions formed from long observation, but
  that's not for here.

  Jerry Clark

  >That's mighty virtuous of you, Ed, and just as meaningless. I
  >can tell you that if you were to try, you'd learn that there's
  >no money worth speaking of to be made in UFO writing anyway.
  >Believe me, I wish that weren't true.
  Jerry,
  I don't have any problem with anyone making money or a living
  from UFO research. I was trying to make the point that I don't
  have a financial reason for exposing myself to the ridicule and
  abuse that I must accept for defending the Alien Autopsy. I
  continue because I believe I have important information to
  contribute to our UFO community. I applaud and respect the work
  of other researchers, but one of the main weaknesses I've
  discovered is their tendency to rush to judgment.
  >In any event, I offer the following citation not for you but for
  >others in the unlikely event that they're still curious about
  >your favorite item of cryptoterrestrial evidence:
  >Philip Mantle, Alien Autopsy Film, R.I.P., International UFO
  >Reporter 32,1 (2008): 15-19.
  Philip and I have been discussing the Alien Autopsy for ten
  years and we do not agree. I think Philip has misread both Ray
  Santilli and his motives and how the various narratives fit
  together. He also has disregarded the crash site evidence, even
  though he knows that it is a very unusual place and everything
  is just as I described. My last word from Philip was: "Ray is
  *#&%ing lying to you gormless sod!"
  So you can see that we don't see eye to eye. Philip has backed
  the story of a magus type of con-man named Spyros. I think
  Spyros is leading Philip down the garden path. There isn't any
  hard evidence that Spyros is telling the truth, in fact
  everything points in the opposite direction. For example, Spyros
  didn't know what lighting he used for the set or very important
  facts about the actual footage. A short list have been
  discussing this for well over a year.
  I realize that many lies and counter-lies have plagued the AA
  footage. But the crash site I've found needs to be studied
  carefully by a team of ufologists.

  defending ourselves from such blanket accusations/denigrations
  as you have posted above - as have others - that little is done
  in the way of discussion.

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m03-003.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:21]
Re: Alien Autopsy Nonsense


  Well you may or may not know I am the guy who produced the
  digitisation of the 1995 Betacam 19 min. copy of the original AA
  footage. This material came directly from Ray Santilli back in
  1995 and contains all the material in circulation of autopsy 2 in
  much fuller form and detail than that seen in network broadcasts
  and Ray's VHS AA video.
  I suspect I've examined every frame of that film in more detail
  than most and when prompted for details Spyros should have known
  about - had he produced the film - was found lacking.
  This problem was further compounded by Spyros's detailed
  discription of the film's production and 'set', it frankly did
  not fit what could be seen in the footage itself, in particular
  a wall with a doorway which can be seen several times in the
  footage but Spyros says he did not build in his 'set' and never
  existed, but it's in the film?.
  The devil is in the detail and Spyros's story does not provide the
  right details about what he claims is his own work, the film itself
  show his claims are inacurate.
  I say inacurate not wrong because I and the other members of the
  List are quite convinced Spyros _was_ involved with Ray. In fact
  Ray provided evidence of this himself _but_ he has not
  provided any cast iron proof, so far, to back-up his primary claim
  that he produced the AA film. In fact one piece of "documentary
  proof" he provided early on in our exchanges turned out to be a
  complete fabrication!
  Philip, why didn't red flags and alarm bells go off when this
  happened?, Everybody else heard them.

  Best regards,
  Neil

UFO Chronicles' Appeal




                                                         UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > May > May 3

                                                        UFO Chronicles' Appeal
  From: Frank Warren <frank-warren.nul>
  Date: Sun, 02 May 2010 11:33:47 -0700
  Archived: Mon, 03 May 2010 06:45:32 -0400
  Subject: UFO Chronicles' Appeal

  To Our Readers, Supporters & Contributors: Thank You!
  Per protocol, at the end of each month (and the beginning of the
  next) I give a shout out to that month's financial donors (as
  well as those who've contributed in the past). In that vein, as
  we begin the month of May, I would like to extend a heart-felt
  thank you to _all_ of our readers, writers and supporters, and
  especially those that have graciously submitted a monetary
  contribution in these difficult financial times.
  From our meager start in January of 2005 we have grown to boast
  readers in over 150 countries, have over 2600 articles archived
  and some of the brightest minds in Ufology have contributed to
  our efforts via penscript, articles, op-eds etc. We've expanded
  to include video, audio and our own radio show is currently in
  the works. Lastly, and not to forget, this is a researcher's
  enterprise, and accordingly, we are always engaged in said
  research and investigation into the UFO phenomenon and various
  cases.
  What began as a blog for myself and colleagues to put the
  proverbial pen to paper, as well as being a repository for
  current and historical UFO reports, has grown into a full-
  fledged e-zine and with that evolution comes the corresponding
  labor, time invested and costs to manage it.
  As this became a full-time job (in the second year) and
  readership was continuing to grow exponentially, many suggested
  that it was time to line up some sponsors, and although that
  notion hasn't been abandoned completely it isn't something that
  I warmly embrace as your editor; largely because a site
  cluttered with ads just doesn't set well with me.
  Henceforth, the decision was made to experiment with donations
  and give readers and supporters a portal to offer monetary
  contributions; the notion, given the high volume of readers,
  was/is to receive a modest stipend from a small percentage of
  those and that would more then suffice.
  From the onset the response was immediate, and some donations
  were gratefully substantial, and interestingly a large
  percentage was from (and continue to be) academics in different
  parts of the world. Although the monthly tallies are increasing
  from month to month they are slight and fall short of the ideal
  goal.
  We Need Your Help
  This year we will continue to expand the site, by adding
  additional writers, as well as installing UFO reporting sections
  from different geographical parts of the globe (published in
  real time), and as mentioned above, we will be lending our voice
  (literally) to the podcast-sphere and or Internet radio. To that
  end, we need your support:
  Let's Start May Out With A Bang!
  To keep this site free from advertisements (to quote another
  great site) if you get something out of The UFO Chronicles, and


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m03-006.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:22]
UFO Chronicles' Appeal

  if you can afford to do so, please give a little back; no amount
  is to small, and large amounts are humbly welcome.
  Click on the link below to donate via PayPal.
  We wish everyone a joyous month of May!
  Sincerely,
  Frank Warren
  Editor/Publisher
  The UFO Chronicles


  THE UFO CHRONICLES
  http://www.theufochronicles.com/
  The Ufo Chronicles Feed
  http://feeds.feedburner.com/TheUFOChronicles
  Help Support The Site
  https://www.paypal.com/cgi-bin/webscr?cmd=_s-xclick&hosted_button_id=7266885

  myself into a position to repeat your errors does not keep me up
  nights.

  Date: Sun, 02 May 2010 21:04:58 +0100
  Archived: Mon, 03 May 2010 06:54:54 -0400

  You seem to be of the opinion Ed that we should invest time,
  effort, and money following up any rumour of something that
  might be an alien spaceship, no matter how unreliable the source
  is.
  No wonder Ufology has got as far as it has today - hardly
  anywhere - when there is a near-total lack of selectivity as to
  where effort and resources should be applied.
  Jerry, 'gormless' is an adjective used to describe someone
  lacking common sense. 'Sod' is a noun applied to someone who is
  so gormless that they are unable to correctly identify an
  orifice appropriate for human reproduction.

  Cheers,
  Joe

                                                             Re: Gormless Sod
  From: John Harney <magonia.nul>
  Date: Sun, 2 May 2010 21:09:29 +0100
  Archived: Mon, 03 May 2010 06:56:38 -0400
  Subject: Re: Gormless Sod

  >From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Sun, 2 May 2010 12:07:41 -0400
  >Subject: Re: Alien Autopsy Nonsense
  >>From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
  >>To: <post.nul>
  >>Date: Sun, 2 May 2010 08:20:18 -0700
  >>Subject: Re: Alien Autopsy Nonsense
  >>Philip and I have been discussing the Alien Autopsy for ten
  >>years and we do not agree. I think Philip has misread both Ray
  >>Santilli and his motives and how the various narratives fit
  >>together. He also has disregarded the crash site evidence, even
  >>though he knows that it is a very unusual place and everything
  >>is just as I described. My last word from Philip was: "Ray is
  >>lying to you gormless sod!"
  >"Gormless sod"?
  >Can any British Listfolk offer a translation?
  "Gorm", or "gaum" is a dialect word, meaning (according to
  Chambers English Dictionary) notice, heed, regard, attention:
  understanding. It is derived from Old Norse "gaumr", heed,
  attention. Hence, "gormless", or "gaumless", clumsy: stupid,
  witless.
  "Sod" is an abbreviation of sodomite, which has obviously lost
  its original meaning. It is normally used as a term of vulgar
  abuse, as in "Miserable sod!", or "Stupid sod!"

  John Harney

  <snip>
  >I can understand your exasperation, Joe, but while you're right
  >on the main point - that the AA film is an absurd fabrication -
  >you're wrong on a couple of levels.
  >First of all, ufologists are no different from anybody else - I
  >could cite numerous examples just off the top of my head (but
  >won't for fear of starting another kind of flame war) - in
  >sometimes holding to dubious and discredited beliefs. Ufology,
  >contrary to another kind of mythology promoted by another self-
  >interested, self-validating subculture, is not some unique
  >social pathology, as you seem to have it. It's just a group of
  >human beings behaving like a group of human beings.
  I think you have rather a selective, idealised concept of what
  ufology is or isn't. Going way back, 'ufology' has actively
  encouraged 'alternative' viewpoints to take part, and has become
  an amorphous mass of a wide range of beliefs which includes
  elements of spiritualism, mass-paranoia, evangelism, paganism,
  and yes, a relatively small clique of serious researchers and
  investigators. It has become a magnet for nearly every kind of
  nutty belief.
  The difference ufology, and say, paganism, is that paganism
  doesn't attempt to assimilate all of the other garbage - it has
  an element of selectivity. Just examine the distinction and even
  animosity between Pagans and Wiccans, for example.
  >Second, at least here in the U.S., the AA film made hardly
  >impact on ufologists. Ed's is an island of gullibility in a sea
  >of skepticism. Where the AA film had an impact - in other words,
  >where it was taken seriously - was among the non-ufologist mass
  >TV-viewing public. (I learned that from personal experience in
  >the town where I live.) As a general principle, in American
  >ufology the AA film hardly rose to the level of "controversy" .
  >It was more like the level of head-shaking.
  The picture is not quite as 'clean' as you paint - how many
  presentations at UFO conferences in the US have featured the AA?

  Regards,
  Joe

  Amendment Room from 10 to 11 am. Important announcements
  emerging from the X-Conference and other breaking news will be
  made at that time.

  staffers have been invited to attend the conference and press
  conference at the NPC. On April 20 at last year's NPC press
  conference Apollo 14 Astronaut Dr. Edgar Mitchell and Air Force
  Major Milton Torres (ret.) called for the Obama administration
  to end the government imposed truth embargo regarding the
  extraterrestrial issue. Dr. Mitchell was following in the
  footsteps of Mercury Astronaut Gordon Cooper who once called
  upon the UN to do same.
  Topics will include: government imposed truth embargo;
  Rockefeller Initiative; UFO/ET connections to the Obama
  administration; the Cydonia/Martian Codex; Disclosure; RAF
  Bentwaters case; Eisenhower briefing memo; UFOs and the National
  Security State; police sightings database; developments in the
  UK; extraordinary developments in Brazil; UFO/ET connections to
  Russia and the Soviet Union; and more.
  Television journalist and former CNN news anchor Cheryll Jones
  will host. Speakers will include: George Noory of Coast to Coast
  AM (Special Luncheon Presentation); George Haas and William
  Saunders (Luncheon Presentation); Stephen Bassett; Richard
  Dolan; A. J. Gevaerd (Brazil); Gary Heseltine (UK); Linda
  Moulton Howe with UK Bentwaters witness John Burroughs; George

The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists [was: AA




                                                         UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > May > May 4

                                      The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists [was: AA
  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Mon, 3 May 2010 09:43:14 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 04 May 2010 08:19:25 -0400
  Subject: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists [was: AA

  >From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  >To: post.nul

  >Subject: Re: Alien Autopsy Nonsense
  >>From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  >>To: <post.nul>
  >>Date: Sun, 2 May 2010 10:00:42 -0400
  >>Subject: Re: Alien Autopsy Nonsense

  >>you're wrong on a couple of levels.
  >>First of all, ufologists are no different from anybody else - I
  >>could cite numerous examples just off the top of my head (but
  >>won't for fear of starting another kind of flame war) - in
  >>sometimes holding to dubious and discredited beliefs. Ufology,
  >>contrary to another kind of mythology promoted by another self-
  >>interested, self-validating subculture, is not some unique
  >>social pathology, as you seem to have it. It's just a group of
  >>human beings behaving like a group of human beings.
  >I think you have rather a selective, idealised concept of what
  >ufology is or isn't. Going way back, 'ufology' has actively
  >encouraged 'alternative' viewpoints to take part, and has become
  >an amorphous mass of a wide range of beliefs which includes
  >elements of spiritualism, mass-paranoia, evangelism, paganism,
  >and yes, a relatively small clique of serious researchers and
  >investigators. It has become a magnet for nearly every kind of
  >nutty belief.
  Thanks for telling me how na=EFve I am, and letting me know I'm
  surrounded by crazy people, have been for decades, and lack the
  wit to figure it out. I guess I must be more stupid than I like
  to think.
  In the real world, the nature of ufology and ufologists is one
  area on which I can claim as much authority as anybody in the
  English-speaking universe. It's not just that I grew up with
  ufology, though I did. My first interaction with the subculture
  was in, as I recall, 1961. Were you even born then?
  Over the course of my life, I have met personally nearly all of
  the significant figures (including first-generation
  personalities such as Keyhoe, Stringfield, Bloecher, Davis, the
  Lorenzens, and more); some of them I came to know quite well. I
  also interacted with innumerable insignificant ones and many who
  were not quite either. Over all that time, I've met relatively
  few crazy people whose mentality took or takes them far outside
  the mainstream. Lazy stereotypes aside, your average ufologist
  is about as colorful as your average neighbor, from whom he or
  she is indistinguishable. I used to say that your average
  ufologist is a middle-aged Republican, though these days there
  seem to be more Democrats around than there once were.
  Anyway, I've read an enormous amount of UFO literature, more - I
  suspect - than you, probably by a considerable margin, including
  out-of-print books and defunct publications you have never heard


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m04-001.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:27]
The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists [was: AA

  of. I have engaged in massive correspondence over the decades
  with UFO personalities. Not only that, but while researching my
  encyclopedia series, I spent a whole lot of time in the archives
  studying reams of private correspondence from notables and not-
  so-notables going back to the early 1950s. So please don't
  lecture me about naivete and ignorance. In turn, I won't lecture
  you about your lack of knowledge and expertise, or your hostile,
  apparently ideologically driven belief that ufology represents
  some unique form of social pathology.
  >The difference ufology, and say, paganism, is that paganism
  >doesn't attempt to assimilate all of the other garbage - it has
  >an element of selectivity. Just examine the distinction and even
  >animosity between Pagans and Wiccans, for example.
  How paganism gets into the discussion is, I confess, beyond me,
  unless your intention is to further marginalize ufology. Which,
  of course, it is. Your contempt for virtually everybody who has
  a sympathetic interest in the UFO phenomenon is affecting the
  quality of your analysis, I'm afraid.
  Your definition of ufologist is apparently "anybody who has any
  interest in UFOs, especially if that person is crazy." Of
  course, since ufology involves no certification process, anybody
  can call himself a ufologist. I'm more interested in what his
  would-be colleagues would call him. The long history of internal
  criticism within ufology tells me he would probably be judged
  crazy and avoided. (Try to find that internal criticism in the
  ranks of ufology's critics, by the way, and you get a clear
  sense of who is more intellectually serious about the truth-
  seeking process.)
  My definition derives from individuals active in the
  organizations (CUFOS, MUFON, CSI, APRO et al.) or on their own
  as investigators, writers, regular attendees of conferences et
  al. These tend to be persons who have some broad understanding,
  if not always a sophisticated one, of the role of science and
  empiricism in inquiry. If I thought you were interested, I'd
  encourage you to read what I wrote about the rich history of
  America (and other) ufology in my encyclopedia. Like other human
  beings, ufologists are far from perfect, and examples of
  foolishness can always be found, but those limitations and
  problems are not explained by craziness or pathology.
  Following the religious-studies scholar J. Gordon Melton, I
  differentiate between ufologists - who, whatever they suspect
  about what ultimate explanations may reveal (in other words, the
  role of theory vs. the certainty of established fact), hold that
  for now we are dealing with unidentified phenomena - and
  saucerians, who take a broadly occult-religious (or, in some
  cases, demonological) perspective. From the latter perspective,
  there are no UFOs, only flying saucers piloted by known
  entities. That is why ufologists and saucerians historically did
  not get along and the former ostracized the latter. (NICAP
  actively warred against contactees during the Keyhoe years, for
  instance.) The two sides had a radically different, almost
  mutually incomprehensible outlook on the phenomenon's meaning
  and definition. (Yes, I know that in England, there were a few
  exceptions, notably FSR editor Waveney Girvan, who championed
  Adamski, though Girvan was more discriminating than most
  contactee advocates.)
  I note that in a posting concurrent with this one, incidentally,
  you seem to think Ed Gehrman's advocacy of the AA film is fueled
  by his desire to find proof of _extraterrestrials_. This may
  explain a few things about why your insight into ufologists in
  general is so askew. Maybe you just aren't listening.

  Jerry Clark

                                                                Re: Journalism
  From: Cathy Reason <CathyM.nul>
  Date: Mon, 3 May 2010 23:34:40 +0100
  Archived: Tue, 04 May 2010 08:24:10 -0400
  Subject: Re: Journalism

  >From: Nick Pope <nick.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Sun, 2 May 2010 21:03:20 +0200 (CEST)
  >Subject: Re: Journalism
  <snip>
  >>Which is style and which is content?
  >Both.
  As a reply to my question that makes as much sense as "lobsters"
  or "Michael Gove's trousers".
  >Unfortunately:
  >Content: wrong. A couple of ufologists called me a clerk,
  >because they dishonestly wanted to downplay my role. MoD nailed
  >ther lie:
  >http://www.nickpope.net/limits-of-a-paper-trail.htm

  Your testimonial from the MOD (extracted from your own website)
  is as follows:
  "From 1991 to 1994 Mr Pope worked as a civil servant within
  Secretariat (Air Staff). He undertook a wide range of
  secretariat tasks relating to central policy, political and
  parliamentary aspects of non-operational RAF activity. Part of
  his duties related to the investigation of unidentified aerial
  phenomena reported to the Department to see if they had any
  defence significance".
  Chambers defines a clerk as "a person employed to deal with
  correspondence, accounts, records etc in an office..." Which
  activities described in the above testimonial definitely do not
  fall within this sphere?
  Before we go off into a complete side-issue here, I'm not really
  interested in down-playing or up-playing your role at the MOD,
  but in illustrating how facts can be manipulated through the use
  of subtext.
  >Style: amateurish.

  where, exactly, style ended and content began, since you insist
  these are completely different things. I'm still waiting.
  Grade: X
  (Candidate failed to turn up for the examination)

  From: Terry Colvin <fortean1.nul>

  Subject: A Search For Anomalies

  Here is the straight poop on Anomaly Register, taken from
  Corliss' Science Frontiers Book Supplement* No. 184, Jul-Aug
  2009. It is lengthy but worth the effort of typing in, and I
  quote:
  QUOTE:
  -----
  Anomaly Register. A massive developing compendium of definitive
  descriptions of roughly 10,000 scientific anomalies and related
  '"provocative"' phenomena. This serial is issued bimonthly. Each
  issue is 16 pages long, with ample illustrations.
  Our prototype is the book (listed above) Scientific Anomalies
  And Other Provocative Phenomena. This 300-page book is an
  *annotated list* of about 6,000 items then on file. In Anomaly
  Register, we add 4,000 new files to the 6,000 entries; plus more
  details, specific examples, illustrations, and back all with
  selected references from the science literature.
  Anomaly Register is designed to portray the *scope* of the
  anomaly universe. Our other serial, Science Frontiers, is aimed
  at the best of many anomalies in the *current* scientific
  journals and magazines we survey. The 22 CATALOGS listed above
  are reserved for *detailed* examples of anomalies, their
  histories, interpretations, and the many thousands of pertinent
  references presently in our files.
  In its first year, Anomaly Register (AR#1-#6) registered 835
  items, with 200 illustrations. All summarized in 96 8x11 unbound
  pages which you can punch for a loose-leaf collection or
  otherwise bind as you wish.
  You'll never find all this information arranged logically, on
  the web - even with [the] best search engine.
  Subscription Prices:
  For our second year, beginning with the August 2009 issue
  (AR#7-AR#12): subscriptions are: $18 (in U.S.); $20 (in Canada);
  $26 (overseas) All by airmail.
  Always available: first-year issues (AR#1-AR#6): $16 (in U.S.);
  where we can we save postage by mailing all 6 issues at the same
  time. Canadian and overseas customers should treat AR#1-AR#6
  (96 pages long) as a book. (Refer to Order Blank for postage
  estimates). We will send Credit memos for any overpayment.
  Order From:
  The Sourcebook Project
  P.O. Box 107
  Glen Arm, MD 21057
  Methods Of Payment:
  All prices are in U.S. dollars. For Canadian dollars, UK
  pounds, Euros, use current exchange rates.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m04-004.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:28]
A Search For Anomalies


  Accepted Foreign Checks:
  Canadian (*including* postal money orders) must be in Canadian
  $$s, UK checks in pounds; these must be drawn on Canadian and UK
  banks and made out to William R. Corliss. Euro checks not
  accepted yet.
  Accepted Currencies:
  US, Canadian, UK, Euros. (Registered mail recommended)
  Please jot down the names and addresses of persons who might
  like to receive [a] free copy of *Science Frontiers*."
  -----
  End Quote
  Corliss does not use e-mail or facsimile (FAX). A friend provides
  him a web presence.
  The primary portal is:
  http://www.science-frontiers.com/
  The Science Frontiers bimonthly newsletters on-line link is:
  http://www.science-frontiers.com/sfonline.htm
  This will allow you to browse and see his older issues.
  The Anomaly Register follows a similar format to Science
  Frontiers; however, much more detailed. For example, Anomaly
  register #9 (January 2010) devoted ten pages to oil and coal
  anomalies.

  >>animosity between Pagans and Wiccans, for example.

  "dubious and discredited beliefs". I used Paganism as an example
  to point out the difference - that the other beliefs are more
  (appropriately) selective when it comes to assimilation of other
  "dubious and discredited beliefs" compared to ufology.
  I realise that recall can be impaired by the advance in age, or
  perhaps your vast library doesn't include works by John Michell
  or the similarly-named Aime Michel, or various others who have
  speculated links between ancient pagan sites and UFOs. I could
  stretch the examples to include the likes of Von D=E4niken, Andrew
  Tomas, or even the Lorenzens. Perhaps you even missed all of the
  articles of a similar nature in FSR and even the journal of one
  example held up by you as evidence of 'rational ufology', MUFON.
  Selective or faulty recall?
  I could go on citing examples of the broadening field of
  'mainstream' ufology through the years, but I feel sure that you
  would claim all of them to be exceptions.
  <snip>

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m05-001.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:30]
Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists

  ufology subculture. Most American ufologists have never heard of
  Michell. As you would not know (not that this ever stops you),
  citations of his work and ideas in the American-ufology
  literature are rare.
  Aime Michel certainly was a ufologist, and a good one, who on
  infrequent occasion indulged in speculation about ET visitation
  in ancient times. You know, just as Carl Sagan did. Many writers
  from many points of view, including countless entirely
  mainstream ones, have offered views of the significance of pagan
  archaeological sites. I guess that according to McConagle logic,
  that makes them all pagans. Do you check for pagans under your
  bed before you retire for the night?
  >I could >stretch the examples to include the likes of Von
  >D=E4niken, Andrew Tomas, or even the Lorenzens. Perhaps you even
  >missed all of the articles of a similar nature in FSR and even
  >the journal of one example held up by you as evidence of
  >'rational ufology', MUFON.
  Von Daniken was not a ufologist, and ufologists did not receive
  him well. If you wish to educate yourself on the matter - which,
  frankly, I doubt; it would just spoil your fun - you might read
  Ancient Astronauts In The UFO Literature, UFO Encyclopedia 2nd
  ed., pp. 75-86.
  The crack about MUFON is predictable. To more informed and
  experienced observers than you, MUFON exemplifies both ufology's
  strengths and its weaknesses, perhaps more dramatically than any
  major American UFO organization. MUFON has done excellent work,
  and it's also indulged misguided, even silly notions, some
  downright embarrassing, none rising to the level of pathology.
  That's why, Joe, I wrote last time around - I guess your brain
  filters out what it doesn't want to recall - that "examples of
  foolishness [in ufology] can always be found." Just as in UFO
  debunking or, for that matter, in all of human activity. Oh,
  excuse me, my brain being old, it forgot for a moment that
  ufology represents unique social pathology.
  >Selective or faulty recall?
  You may well ask yourself.
  >I could go on citing examples of the broadening field of
  >'mainstream' ufology through the years, but I feel sure that you
  >would claim all of them to be exceptions.
  You don't know very much, do you?

  Jerry Clark

  >I think you have rather a selective, idealised concept of what
  >ufology is or isn't. Going way back, 'ufology' has actively
  >encouraged 'alternative' viewpoints to take part, and has become
  >an amorphous mass of a wide range of beliefs which includes
  >elements of spiritualism, mass-paranoia, evangelism, paganism,
  >and yes, a relatively small clique of serious researchers and
  >investigators. It has become a magnet for nearly every kind of
  >nutty belief.
  >The difference ufology, and say, paganism, is that paganism
  >doesn't attempt to assimilate all of the other garbage - it has
  >an element of selectivity. Just examine the distinction and even
  >animosity between Pagans and Wiccans, for example.
  That's total nonsense. It would be like saying "Just examine the
  distinction and even animosity between Christians and
  Methodists, for example."
  Given that many in the UFO field have trouble understanding both
  the broad ideas and nuances of the major monotheistic religions,
  randomly bringing in minority religions is sure to muddy the
  waters even further.
  What is clear, however, is that calling the religious practice
  of any List members "garbage" is offensive and inappropriate.

  Carol Maltby

   >All the strange and unusual UFO happenings can

   object over Yukon Territory, Canada, On December 11th, 1996

   in November, 1986

   "greys" (different types), human like, "praying mantis" type and
   reptilian type.

   http://www.nicap.org/Occupant.htm
   http://tinyurl.com/326ruj9

   ships"?

   cryptoterrestrials? So we can assume that on the Earth had
   developer at least 4 more types of intelligent beings
   independently from us? I doubt this is possible.
   However I believe there are another intelligent beings on the
   Earth, which are not us and not extraterrestrials. I have
   mentioned this in my previous email and later I will bring some
   material which, I think, will back up this idea.

  What you have read dear Listers above is - IMHO - an example of
  two well respected and informed Ufologists displaying how _not_
  to conduct a debate on a forum such as this...
  I for one am fed up to the eye teeth with this kind of personal
  bickering full of insult and sarcasm, so prevalant on UpDates
  these days.
  I have seen it so many times and for me it spoils the content.
  Is it not possible guys - and I ask this question to others on
  here who use similar tactics - to debate and argue points
  without personal remarks about senility, naiveity, stupidity,
  contempt etc etc? It gets very wearing and is totally
  unnecessary.
  I am sure this kind of behaviour is also intimidating to some of
  us who would like to make valid points but are reluctant to have
  to be forced into a defensive position after being flamed on a
  personal level.

                                           Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists
  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Wed, 5 May 2010 09:50:14 -0400
  Archived: Wed, 05 May 2010 11:09:54 -0400
  Subject: Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists

  >From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>

  >The message is clear. No-one who expresses views outside
  >Jerry's comfort zone are considered as ufologists by him.
  Patient and gentle Listfolk:
  Let us summarize here.
  This whole bizarre exchange commenced when, with na=EFve good
  intentions, I offered McGonagle a chance to clarify his meaning.
  As we know, we all sometimes write without sufficient care and
  precision, exposing ourselves to readings we did not intend. I
  afforded Joe the opportunity to make clear he does not hold the
  position that ufology represents an unprecedented form of social
  pathology. That is, of course, a point of view so manifestly
  crazy that one, or at least I, would not expect it from someone
  whom, till now, I had regarded - apparently, again, naively -
  as a reasonable individual.
  It turns out that JMcG does indeed believe ufology amounts to a
  unique form of social pathology. Obviously, this view is wrong
  on two levels: the uniqueness and the pathology. As I observed -
  as I thought, uncontroversially - ufologists behave in
  recognizable, predictable human ways. As I've remarked
  elsewhere, a woman who raises sled dogs in northern Minnesota
  once observed to me that temperamentally and behaviorally, the
  subculture of ufologists is indistinguishable from the
  subculture of sled-dog raisers.
  Unfortunately, Joe advances his argument - if "argument" isn't
  elevating what seems more accurately characterized as an
  irritation of temperament - by pretending that anybody who has
  ever expressed any interest, preferably a crazy one, in UFOs (or
  anything conceivably related to them; Joe's definition is broad)
  is a ufologist.
  Seeking to steer him toward a more reality-based analysis, I
  tried to explain that, to start with, ufologists don't own UFOs.
  Second, by definition ufologists are - duh - participants in the
  ufology subculture, which has a clear history going back to the
  early 1950s (though arguably rooted in the earlier Fortean
  Society) and which interested persons can document in the
  voluminous record of organizations, clubs, newsletters,


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m05-009.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:34]
Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists

  magazines, research, investigation, letters, and books by
  activists (of varying degrees of energy, productivity, and
  influence) who interact with other activists.
  Moreover, to pretend that ufology is a unique pathology,
  fundamentally different from any ordinary human group behavior,
  our correspondent has to redefine ufology into something nobody
  who knows anything about the ufology subculture would recognize.
  And then he insults anyone who refuses to rush after him down
  the yellow brick road he has constructed out of his imagination.
  We are left to ponder a couple of possibilities: (1) Joe has
  missed the irony in the Orwellian dictum "ignorance is
  strength." Or (2) perhaps he's trying to start up his own
  uniquely pathological approach, drawing on the premise that
  since ufologists are uniquely contemptible by definition, one
  doesn't have to know much about them to trash them.

  Sigh,
  Jerry Clark

  If you're that sensitive, Dave, I would strongly encourage you
  never to read the letters section of the New York Review of
  Books, ordinarily considered America's leading journal of
  letters. I confess, however, that I don't recall ever seeing one
  correspondent accuse another of near-senility, but it's possible
  I missed that one. I've certainly seen no shortage of blunt and
  even rude exchanges.
  Exchanges of views - which are not always mere "bickering" - in
  any forum, including the most elevated ones (see above), are
  often rough and tumble, Dave. For a more rounded understanding
  of how these things work, I'd encourage you to expand your
  reading beyond those catering simply to those interested in
  UFOs. As the cliche goes, if you can't stand the heat, stay out
  of the kitchen.

  Cordially,
  Jerry Clark

  Conservative Party member John Rimmer breaks his eerie silence!

  "Seeking to steer [one J. McGonagle] toward a more reality-based
  analysis, I tried to explain that, to start with, ufologists
  don't own UFOs.
  Second, by definition ufologists are - duh - participants in the
  ufology subculture, which has a clear history going back to the
  early 1950s (though arguably rooted in the earlier Fortean
  Society) and which interested persons can document in the
  voluminous record of organizations, clubs, newsletters,
  magazines, research, investigation, letters, and books by
  activists (of varying degrees of energy, productivity, and
  influence) who interact with other activists."
                            --Senile ufologist J. Clark,
                            writing on May 5, 2010, in UFO UpDates


  Jerry Clark

  someone needs to wade into the Cartesian Ideologue's morass and
  apply some judicious backhands, eh? Moreover, a little more of

  demonstrates more intellectual bravery? Which uses more science

Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists

  dependable?
  Contrarily, which one uses mockery, ad hominem, character
  assassination, intellectual fallacy, and obfuscation? Which is
  the dimensionless and dogmatic ideologue, and which is the
  inspired idealist? Which hounds and which is hounded?
  Further, which is deliberately abusive and which is
  sarcastically accusative? Which won't address the points already
  made, and which will concede the point well made? Which one
  follows the data and which one cherry-picks it?
  I think you might begin to see how instructive an exchange, even
  one such as this, can remain to be, eh?
  I say all this remembering how long you and I have had this
  discussion. You know how much I genuinely respect same. Still,
  it remains the opposition, by and large, won't credit that same
  respect and only take it for weakness to be exploited. Hail to
  the Chief, Jerry Clark, for standing up and very obviously
  standing a sneer down in a deliberate, unequivocating , and
  intelligent manner.
  Best to you, Brother Dave.

                                    04-28-10 NASA Astrobiology Teleconference Audio
  From: Giuliano Marinkovic <giuliano.marinkovic.nul>
  Date: Wed, 5 May 2010 18:20:47 +0000 (GMT)
  Archived: Thu, 06 May 2010 10:48:54 -0400
  Subject: 04-28-10 NASA Astrobiology Teleconference Audio

  Dear colleagues.
  Washington Post has published yesterday this article:
  -----
  NASA Team Cites New Evidence That Meteorites From Mars Contain Ancient Fossils
  Source: Washington Post
  http://tinyurl.com/2497fxb
  Recap: Team reopened a 14-year-old controversy on
  extraterrestrial life last week, reaffirming and offering
  support for its widely challenged assertion that a 4-billion-
  year-old meteorite that landed thousands of years ago on
  Antarctica shows evidence of microscopic life on Mars.
  -----
  Also on the same note - as promised in my recent posts I have
  uploaded recording of the audio stream from NASA teleconference
  on astrobiology from April 28, 2010.
  Streaming was choppy and unstable at the beginning so that is
  the reason why sometimes audio is coming on and off - later the
  streaming was more stable so I was able to archive almost the
  whole conference.
  This is the link for download of audio - exclusively here:
  http://tinyurl.com/2ayr47t
  After you click on the link new page will open and there simply
  click on the blue word "Here" for direct download of the mp3 file.
  Questions were open for journalist somewhere around 18th minute.
  First question came from Seth Borenstein from Associated Press
  who was, besides general astrobiology projects, more interested
  about official NASA policy on "broadcasting into the universe"
  in the sense of recent Stephen Hawking's statement on "alien
  treat".
  A day later (April 29, 2010) Borenstein published this article:
  Too Risky To Phone Et? Too Late - Nasa's tried it
  http://www.msnbc.msn.com/id/36855256/ns/technology_and_science-space/
  Other articles that were published after the conference:
  http://tinyurl.com/325dn5t
  http://tinyurl.com/3ylrk62
  General information about the event:
  http://www.astrobiology.com/news/viewpr.html?pid=30699

  Alfred, I can see of course how one can weigh the arguments of
  folk with opposing views by judging one weaker because he or she
  resorts to personal attacks but I would rather see the opponents
  impressing on account of their various intellectual points made.
  There's nothing I enjoy more than a right 'ding dong' over some
  controversy but feel strongly that it's better for an issue or
  argument to win rather than the person who makes it.
  In simple terms it's OK for me to tell you that I think what you
  have said is idiotic but counterproductive to our ongoing
  conversation by saying you're an idiot.

  John a 'bad boy' but instead pointing out he'd done a bad thing.

                                           Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists
  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Thu, 06 May 2010 03:42:43 +0100
  Archived: Thu, 06 May 2010 11:10:27 -0400
  Subject: Re: The Nature Of Ufology And Ufologists

  I'll try one more time. Let's compare the 'UFO-subculture' with
  the 'fairy subculture' based on the literature.
  In the fairy literature, how many of the following appear?
  - animal mutilations
  - encounters with fairies uncovered by HR
  - abductions (that is one)
  - claims that the fairies built the Pyramids or Stonehenge
  - a global cover-up about the existence of fairies
  - a human-usable technology for free energy
  - fairies forcibly impregnate women then steal the foetuses
  - faked official documents
  - accusations that the British Royal family are fairies
  - a catalogue of 57 varieties of fairy
  - a fairy crash/retrieval
  - fairies (okay that's another match)
  - people have been killed because they knew too much about fairies
  - fairies are engaged in global monitoring of the population
  - telepathic contact with fairies
  - remote viewers touring fairy cities
  - fairies shadowed the moon-missions
  - there is a global media blackout on the topic of fairies
  - fairies are concerned about nuclear warfare
  - fairies shot down 1,000 US war planes
  - fairies created a global network of energy lines
  - fairies are all-powerful benevolent beings (another one)
  - fairies are malevolent beings
  - the SDI was set up to defend us against fairies
  - fairies are our progenitors
  - fairies can defy physical laws (another one)
  - crop circles are made by fairies (possibly another one)
  - military jets have been sent to intercept fairies
  - Jesus Christ was a fairy
  - transistors are fairy technology
  - the fairy autopsy film
  - faked fairy photographs (there's another one)
  - a fairy was present on 9/11
  - and so on, and so on, and so on. The list is not exhaustive.
  Now replace the fairy belief with belief in the Loch Ness
  monster - how many of the above beliefs have been incorporated
  into the literature on that topic from the above list?
  Replace fairies with almost any other supernatural belief and
  you will find that nearly all of them apply a filter to imported
  beliefs, unlike ufology.
  Where you do find cross-sub-cultural beliefs, most of these have
  been assimilated over hundreds, if not thousands of years.
  Ufology had assimilated more beliefs from other cultures in
  what, 70 years?
  Still I suppose all of the examples cited are exceptions. A lot
  of them, aren't there?
  And no, Carol, I haven't called belief in fairies garbage - in
  fact there is far less garbage in paganism or fairyism than
  there is in ufology.

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m07-004.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:58]
Re: City On Mars?




                                                         UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > May > May 7

                                                             Re: City On Mars?
  From: Carol Maltby <carolmaltby.nul>
  Date: Thu, 6 May 2010 13:11:56 -0400
  Archived: Fri, 07 May 2010 08:12:39 -0400
  Subject: Re: City On Mars?

  >From: Joe Faccenda <Uforth.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Wed, 5 May 2010 19:43:21 EDT
  >Subject: City On Mars?
  >I have a compilation at:
  >http://www.uforth.com/bird%20skull.htm
  <snip>
  First of all, a recommendation for all presentations based on
  satellite or ground-level Mars images: always include the image
  number and URL where it can be found (or was found in the past,
  in case it disappears, which they sometimes do).
  If it's worth making a presentation on, it's worth knowing what
  you are talking about so that you can answer any rebuttals you
  may receive.
  Number one thing you need to know before getting excited about
  any Mars anomalies is how to distinguish digital artifacts from
  what may be actual features. In this case, that the area that
  looks like a city is made up of strips should be a red flag.
  When you start enlarging it further, you'll see that it totally
  breaks down into a geometric crazy quilt of digital patterns
  that have nothing to do with depicting any natural or artificial
  feature. A look at the YouTube video (warning: electronic music
  abuse ahead!) "Mars City Inside the Hale Crater" will give you a
  vivid example of what not to do.
  http://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uS_K-KAFKJg
  If a Mars "city" looks very ordered in one area, follow it out
  to its "suburbs" and see what happens to it on the edges or
  where it meets incompatible terrain. Sometimes an area that
  looks pretty rectilinear in one section will naturally degrade
  into totally chaotic, obviously natural terrain. Some people
  will just present the most regularly geometric section cropped
  out of its context. That's nothing short of dishonest.
  In the case of the Hale Crater image, see how your little
  rectilinear city features manage to continue up the side of a
  hill as if the terrain going vertical was no barrier at all.
  What's the scale of the "buildings"? It's your responsibility to
  indicate what size your buildings are, so others can determine
  how credible the claim is.
  It's always mandatory to consider sun and camera angles to see
  if they are congruent with your assumptions about what you are
  seeing. If the camera is right overhead, anything that appears
  to be a perspective showing a roof and side walls can be ruled
  out.
  In your Hale Crater image, you've neglected to check the
  directions of the shadows. If we call the source image to be
  oriented with north at the top (for the sake of argument), the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m07-005.shtml[10/12/2011 21:05:59]
Re: City On Mars?

  sun is shining from the southeast with the shadows in the
  northwest. Many of the rectilinear components that look like
  buildings appear to have "shadows" pointing to the south, which
  would mean a light source to the north. Real features don't have
  random light sources. In some cases, checking shadows is what
  you'll need to do to determine whether a feature is a dome or a
  crater.
  Being able to view Mars and examine sections of it closely is
  one of the glories of being alive in this time and place. There
  are some very unusual features worthy of more study, and I don't
  rule out possibilities of some being intelligently constructed.
  But if you are looking for signs of ruined constructed artifacts
  you have to be able to observe accurately, to describe what you
  observe accurately, and to be able to place it in a context that
  rules out natural origin. There are routine objections that
  always come up regarding anomalies. You've got to be able to
  know them, and counter them. I don't think you've made a
  persuasive case here on any level.
  I'd love to have input from archeologists who have training and
  experience in working with satellite images in an archeological
  context, and who are willing to put their names and professional
  credibility on the line when proposing that some Mars features
  may be non-natural. But in the 12 years I've been following Mars
  anomalies, to the best of my knowledge there has not been a
  single example of an archeologist publicly discussing criteria
  for assessing Mars anomalies. I expect that for one of them to
  discuss what the criteria would be for determining whether the
  Face on Mars is a large eroded sculptural form would be
  professional suicide.

  Carol Maltby
