                                                   Rock 'n' Roll, Comics & UFOs
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Sun, 23 May 2010 07:23:13 EDT
  Archived: Sun, 23 May 2010 07:50:10 -0400
  Subject: Rock 'n' Roll, Comics & UFOs

  Recently an entertaining turn of events occurred with some
  people I know out here in Hollywood.
  One of the animation groups decided to host a new mega
  convention featuring comic books, sci-fi, and rock and roll.
  It's going to be at the Pasadena Convention Center May 28-30 and
  they asked me to help them book some guests as I work in the
  comics/animation/sci-fi areas. It's all been last minute
  scrambling for me to help them out but I think we'll do just
  fine.
  I was thinking of whom I could get and remembered Dick Hoagland
  and George Noory and his producer Tom Danheiser as they'd
  mentioned to me they wanted to do more sci-fi/comics
  conventions. So I put out the invitation to them and although
  Dick couldn't make it, George and Tom will. I was hoping to get
  Stanton Friedman too but the distance and time zone and time
  frame was too short. I thought of many of you on this List but
  again the time frame was too short and George and Tom live and
  work not too far from me.
  The reason I wanted to book some folks knowledgeable about UFOs
  is because the sci fi/comics/animation and film industry has
  reaped in trillions of dollars based on the ET phenom and I'd
  seen over the years many UFO researchers not getting the credit
  they were due as big media companies would bite off their
  theories and discoveries and make mints at the box office.
  Well if all goes right this might be a big step in the right
  direction to get the people to meet experts in the UFO field on
  an entertainment venue that crosses many fields. Lots of big
  name acts will be appearing and perhaps it will lead to more
  events like this one which we hope will both entertain and
  inform the public.
  Nothing has been done like this before especially here in the
  greater Los Angeles area so we'll see what shakes from this
  tree.
  I'm hoping it will lead to events where many of you on this List
  can attend and rub elbows with producers and public and get your
  books and projects more in the limelight and under your own
  control instead of some rep or agent or studio bean-counters.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com
  http://www.rockncomiccon.com

  it easier is the internet. Not like the old days of our comic
  book/sci-fi conventions where you would have to take out ads all

  >>begins, "Liberal Senator Colin Kenny was approached by Hellyer
  >>in late 2005..."
  >>According to this article Kenny chaired the Senate defense
  >>committee in Canada. If we take Kenny at his word he seemingly
  >>knows absolutely nothing about the extraterrestrials that are
  >>coming to earth and is absolutely convinced that they are not
  >>here.
  >>I don't know how some of you feel, but what would you think if
  >>President Obama were to go on national television and nominate
  >>someone as Secretary of Defense here in America and his
  >>credentials were that he knows absolutely nothing about the
  >>defense industry, was never in the American military and had no
  >>experience at the position that he was being hired to head. I
  >>think most people would wonder what the heck the president was
  >>doing nominating such an unqualified person for such and
  >>important job.

  >>Senator Colin Kenny actually knows something about the
  >>extraterrestrial phenomena, when as far as I know their is not a
  >>shred of evidence to support his position.
  >The senate in Canada is a chamber of so-called sober second
  >thought that is able to recommend changes to new legislation
  >proposed by the house of commons. It does not pass laws like the
  >US senate. This does not excuse Mr. Kenny's apparent ignorance,
  >but puts his possible influence on public policy in perspective.
  >I think most people in the political arena follow rather than
  >lead public opinion to try to keep the respect of the majority
  >of the population. A comment that ETs are real would be
  >perceived as leading rather than following, hence, is risky.
  >People like Mr. Kenny might expect such a comment to negatively
  >affect their reputations and the reputations of the political
  >institutions they represent. So no one should be surprised at
  >Mr. Kenny's reported opinion that the reality of ETs is "for the
  >birds". That view will likely not change until he perceives
  >mainstream support for such a change.

  An interesting way to look at the situation. I had not thought
  about it that way!

  Lyle Michel

  From: Nick Balaskas <nikolaos.nul>
  Date: Sun, 23 May 2010 23:43:12 -0400
  Archived: Mon, 24 May 2010 07:53:47 -0400
  Subject: Re: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada

  >From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>

  >Subject: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada

  >There are some images on this site's story about another spiral
  >seen in the skies.
  >http://tinyurl.com/2wn7ahd
  >The still images look interesting but the supposed video footage
  >looks CGI no doubt.

  Hi Greg,
  I agree with you - this supposed video footage does look like
  Computer Generated Imagery.
  Having witnessed many bright fireballs and seen many more
  captured with our all-sky cameras, this spiral is certainly not
  a meteor or re-entering space debris.
  Fireballs that I have seen that were much less brighter than
  this spiral lit up the night sky and the surrounding ground
  (even the distant mountains in the places where I once worked).
  This very bright spiral does not do this - or even cast moving
  shadows.
  Bright meteors/bolides often leave persistent smoke/dust trails
  which would be clearly visible in the sky, even at dawn or dusk
  as in this case. This is clearly not the case with this bright
  spiral.
  Although I am not an entomologists, I do like the outdoors and
  enjoy listening to the sounds of field crickets here in Ontario
  when they begin to sing in early summer. Since the southern most
  part of Ontario is further south than the northern border of
  California, I was surprised to hear cricker sounds in this video
  recorded in western Canada where I thought it would be too cold
  for crickets or other singing bugs. Maybe the sound of the
  singing crickets was dubbed for effect. If it wasn't, how come
  they did not capture the loud reported sounds that closely
  followed this incredible sighting?
  There are many other things I would like to point out but it
  would only serve to educate potential hoaxers to make better CGI
  videos.

  Nick Balaskas

  independent photos have surfaced, as well as the 'too-good-to-
  be-true' video. Either all were in on the hoax, or the video was

  photographed details of the spiral suggested that the structure
  was somehow "painted" in the sky, while the video evidence
  showed what appeared to be a spinning, out-of-control rocket.
  Is this similarity a coincidence or does it suggest a conspiracy
  to downplay the significance of oddities in the sky? Are there
  other cases where this may have occurred?

UN Secret Meeting Hoax Exposed




                                                         UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                                 UN Secret Meeting Hoax Exposed
  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Tue, 25 May 2010 01:54:46 +0100
  Archived: Tue, 25 May 2010 07:27:25 -0400
  Subject: UN Secret Meeting Hoax Exposed

  I haven't seen this mentioned on List, apologies if I missed it.
  It was brought to my attention by the noble and modest 'Isaac
  Koi' who has proved to be a meticulous and dedicated ufologist.
  If you cast your mind back to 2008 and a story broke that there
  had been a secret UN meeting about UFOs. The story was touted
  initially by Clay and Shawn Pickering, then taken up by the
  Exocranial brigade, see:
  http://exopolitics.org/Exo-Comment-65.htm
  The first source who the Pickerings appear to have suggested was
  a diplomat at the above link became known as 'Source A' and was
  later described by Michael Salla at:
  http://exopolitics.org/Exo-Comment-80.htm
  as 'a serving US Navy officer willing to discuss sensitive
  information concerning UFOs and extraterrestrial life.'
  Another source emerged, Gilles Lorant, who Salla described as an
  aviation expert with connections to various French scientific
  and military institutes who broadly confirmed the details
  provided by 'Source A'. Gildas Bourdais initially thought that
  Lorant was credible, writing at:
  http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2008/mar/m03-005.shtml
  'I think that the testimony of Gilles Lorant is credible, much
  more than the initial one given through Michael Salla!'
  but his opinion changed very quickly and Bourdais exposed Lorant
  as a fraud at:
  http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2008/mar/m07-004.shtml
  Now a team of intrepid investigators including Stephen Broadbent,
  Andy Murray, and others identified 'Source A' and have
  convincingly debunked his claims and credentials at:
  http://tinyurl.com/24pa5l5
  Michael Salla has attempted a desperate last-ditch defence,
  attempting to draw Bruce Maccabee into the fray at:
  http://news.exopoliticsinstitute.org/index.php/archives/263
  which is countered by another article by Broadbent at:
  http://tinyurl.com/23oyhyo
  Michael - you're looking in the wrong direction, you should be
  chasing fairies - that's where the real conspiracy lies. Believe
  me, I am a security-cleared government fairyologist! Just give
  me $50K and I will give you the proof.

  Joe

                                             Re: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Mon, 24 May 2010 21:05:41 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 25 May 2010 07:28:33 -0400
  Subject: Re: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada

  >From: Gord Heath - UFOBC <gord.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Mon, 24 May 2010 08:16:03 -0700
  >Subject: Re: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada
  >>From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  >>To: post.nul
  >>Date: Mon, 24 May 2010 09:05:15 -0400
  >>Subject: Re: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada
  ><snip>
  >Some clues that this is a hoax?
  >How about for starters that there is no mention of the city
  >which the 'event' was created -- the identity of the city which
  >appears in the reference photo(s) for the Photoshopped images.
  >As far as I can tell there are no reports on any news outlet
  >except this 'Weather News' website which no one has ever heard
  >of.
  >I have still to hear someone, on that most reliable news source
  >Coast to Coast AM, tell us of the 50 anonymous sources from all
  >over Western Canada who saw this spiral in association with
  >flybys by ominous echelons of Chad drones, providing security
  >for the astronauts returning from the Serpo mission because they
  >heard that the Great Lake Dive Company discovered that the F-89
  >missing from Kinross Air Force Base in 1953 was hit by a bottle
  >cap that is lying on the bottom of someone's aquarium.
  The website now reports the identity of the hoaxer, has
  apologized, and has fired the Space News Editor responsible for
  accepting the story.
  The perpetrator says he wanted to test whether Americans were
  more or less gullible than Europeans. He's decided that his hoax
  showed Americans are more gullible.
  I'll bet many of you gullible Americans now believe his story
  about the reason for the hoax.
  But seriously, my dictionary says being gullible merely means
  that one is easy to deceive. This is meaningless without taking
  into account the cost that comes with belief in the deception.
  What we have here is the well-known trade off between hits and
  false alarms in decision theory. We differ in where we set our
  criterion for accepting the presented anomaly as a real event.
  The setting changes all the time depending on the perceived cost
  of making a mistake (i.e., producing a false alarm).
  For most of us, the cost of not recognizing a hoax for a while
  is minimal. It may even be a benefit if it prompts further
  investigation. So there is nothing wrong with gullibility unless
  the cost is high. For some of us, the potential blow to the ego
  from generating a false alarm is sufficient to set a stricter
  criterion for generating a hit.
  So if Americans really are more gullible than Europeans, it may


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m25-003.shtml[10/12/2011 21:06:32]
Re: UFO Spiral Over Western Canada

  simply mean that Europeans have more tender egos. Not that
  there's anything _wrong_ with that!

  William

  What about the fakes?
  Isn't it true that these strange constructions in our cereal
  crops are of human construction? Don't they have to be entirely
  of human construction for the skeptic's position to have _any_
  validity? Just ONE 'genuine' circle, consider, and the game
  a'foot is altogether new!
  And Crop Circles get faked. Sure. But, when those 'fakes'
  occur... don't we, generally, know who the fakers are? Don't
  _they_ tell us who they are? Don't they strut and crow and smirk
  and patronize?
  Aren't they stealthy people haters named "Doug" and "Dave," even
  as these supposed self-aggrandizing psychopaths die off and
  evolve, change their names with the years, upgrade their
  techniques over time, and justify or attempt to legitimize their
  crass sociopathy... as art? Could it be that these offensive
  fakers are behind _all_ the circles? Builders of _all_ the
  constructions? Composers of _all_ these glyphs?
  Yes?
  Then how is it, as Robert Nichol intimates in his compellingly
  sincere film documentary "StarDreams", that these alleged
  'hoaxers' always finish what they start? Where are the failed
  attempts? Where do they 'practice' their completeness of vision,
  this exponential magnitude of astonishing endeavor... this
  meticulous 'perfection' of flawlessly breathtaking execution and


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m27-004.shtml[10/12/2011 21:06:37]
Re: News Links - 23-05-10

  timing. Where are the 'blunders' in these huge constructions,
  extant, otherwise abundantly found in other human executions of
  complicated tasks?
  Why are the 'greater' circles never duplicated? Why don't we see
  a balanced explanation for complex circle construction making it
  into the "National Geographic" or a Postmodern Art periodical.
  Spin me a "Catherine's Wheel", again - 'Doug'! Stomp out an
  additional "Adams Grave," for me - 'Dave'.
  Speaking of art and artists, where _is_ this extremely
  improbable army of gifted performers "without egos" -
  astoundingly selfless and undeniably talented men and women,
  anyway?
  Flatly, these uncharacteristically secretive persons are
  _required_ to execute - without the smallest flaw, remember -
  the delicate artistic balances of line and curve, light and
  shadow... amidst other conventions of accomplished artistry ..
  to include knowledge regarding the sacred or eternal
  geometrical... the strict adherence to the enlightened measure,
  or an educated unity in utility that is an artistic "golden
  mean"... and this over a _huge_ area on an entirely unique
  canvas!
  These 'artists' ARE the real deal... reader And we haven't a
  clue who they really are! No. We're too well rewarded by society
  for not wanting to find out, for not being brave, and for not
  seeking out those clues. We're punished effectively for
  obstinately seeking them too... ...served painful irony in the
  form of reward for negative back-stepping behavior and
  punishment for the positive forward-looking pursuit of same.
  Verily, WHO hoaxes roughly tens of tens of quality circles a
  year, world-wide, for the last ten years? Who operates with no
  professional 'recognition', no lucrative 'pay-off' - no 'reward'
  of any kind? Who?
  Clearly, few to none. Those who do are known to us, ply their
  trade for money, and even have their own trendy flash-fronted
  websites. No... it becomes obvious as one scratches the surface
  of this thing that these people are NOT the whole show.
  Something's hidden behind the official cultural tarp that is our
  corrupted and duplicitous mainstream...
  Moreover, artists produce their work to be associated with it,
  to be defined by it, to be enriched by it... materially AND
  intellectually.
  I'm an artist of small note and legitimate award and I feel
  that's a fair statement. We won't ordinarily BE the artistic
  Samaritan - rare creature, that- who disappears from the scene
  of the artistic 'accident' before they can be celebrated and
  feted by admiring fellow humans. An artist signs his work,
  reader! It's been that way since the renaissance from the middle
  ages, and even before that the interested knew who their artists
  were.
  And those people of the renaissance were baffled by -officially
  intimidated by - CropCircles then too, remember. Crop circles
  have long legs in history.
  Let's cut to the chase. Most circles could be fake, respected
  reader, sure. But MANY are not I suspect! Nichols suggests in
  "StarDreams" that this should, at least, give the reader
  pause... even if it does NOT fuel an ongoing epiphany of
  boundless optimism for the reader... like it does for this
  writer.

  alienview.nul

  >>A bit reminiscent of sectors on a computer disk.
  >Eleanor:
  >What always amazes me is that crop circle chasers think the crop
  >circles are about them, something trying to communicate with
  >them.
  This crop formation appears to encode Euler's Identity in ascii
  code, although there is one character in error.
  http://tinyurl.com/2ufhcws
  The motive for making it may be artistic, since Euler's formula
  is "widely regarded as the most perfect equation in
  mathematics".
  But see the following page for an argument that the formation
  likely contains a second message.
  http://tinyurl.com/2wrzen4
  Attempted interpretations of presumed messages employ symbols
  familiar to us. This is probably unavoidable. But if the
  formations are a form of communication from "them" to "us", why
  would "we" and "they" be using the same symbol set?
  One would have to say that the sender must have learned our
  symbol set in order to create a message from "them" to "us". The
  use of the ascii code is an obvious case where this must be
  true. Ascii code is hardly universal.
  But then there are several anecdotal reports of a person or a
  group of people consciously willing a particular design to
  appear, and one very much like it appearing nearby within a day
  or two. This suggests that human consciousness may somehow be
  involved in the design of an imminent formation, or is
  unknowingly able to precognize the design.
  If there were human involvement in the design process,
  interpreting its meaning using known symbols would then be a


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m28-002.shtml[10/12/2011 21:06:38]
Re: News Links - 23-05-10

  little like dream interpretation.
  >My take? A group of artistic types have figured out how to
  >make art out of a field of crops. It is just art. The artists
  >throw together symbols from various places, as the mood
  >strikes them.
  The formations may have a utilitarian purpose, such as
  communicating an encoded message. But maybe they _are_ just art
  intended to appeal to our sense of aesthetics. For many people,
  they do just that.
  Apparently, some are made using the low-tech rope and board
  technique, and maybe the makers think they are creating art.
  Other formations show the effects of a point source of
  electromagnetic energy, meaning that a more hi-tech approach was
  employed. The motives and identities of the latter makers are
  mainly at issue.

  William

  From: Stanton T Friedman <fsphys.nul>

  Subject: New Book - Science Was Wrong

  I have been asked several times recently when my new book would
  be out. Finally I can report that Science Was Wrong: Startling
  Truths About Cures, Theories And Inventions They Declared
  Impossible. by myself and Kathleen Marden has just been published
  by Career Press.
  We each did 7 of the 14 chapters. One each dealt with UFOs.
  The rest cover such topics as space, aviation, cold fusion, so-
  called climate change, and some disturbing medical stories,
  paranormal phenomena, etc....
  There has been no shortage of instances in which smart people
  said stupid things that stood in the way of technological
  progress and often led to unnecessary deaths and injuries.
  I am hoping to be on Larry King again and also Coast to Coast
  and would be happy to debate any debunkers such as Dr. Paul
  Davies, Seth Shostak, or Stephen Hawking concerning their
  unscientific views about alien visitation.
  Copies will soon be available in bookstores.
  A copy, autographed by both of us, can be obtained via my
  website at:
  www.stantonfriedman.com
  or by sending a check for $19.95 to me at:
  POB 958,
  Houlton,
  ME
  04730-0958
  USA
  That includes Priority mail.

  Respectfully,
  Stan Friedman

Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions &




                                                         UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > May > May 29

                                    Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions &
  From: Jack Brewer <brewer.jack.nul>
  Date: Sat, 29 May 2010 05:21:27 -0700 (PDT)
  Archived: Sat, 29 May 2010 14:06:44 -0400
  Subject: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions &

  Due to the popularity of the topics, I am motivated to present
  the following for consideration:
  In an article titled Physical Evidence Of Alien Abductions May
  Exist In Household Dust, the work of a Dr. William Levengood is
  explored. The link:
  http://www.abduct.com/research/r9.php
  The article states in part:
  "A significant breakthrough in the search for trace physical
  evidence of alien abductions may have been uncovered in ordinary
  household dust. According to biophysicist Dr. William C.
  Levengood of Pinelandia Biophysics Laboratory of Michigan,
  microscopic' . glassy particles' have been found in household
  dust of individuals claiming to be abductees.' Glassy
  particles' have been found in bedrooms, kitchens, family
  rooms, hallways, basement stairs, and so on.
  Glassy particles' have not been found in dust where abductions
  are not known to occur' Dr. Levengood is continuing his research
  into uncovering the origin of the currently unexplainable glassy
  particles." Minimal research makes it quickly apparent that in
  addition to Dr. Levengood's interest in alien abduction which,
  by the way, includes some interesting possible correlations
  between microwave activity and the Walton case, Dr. Levengood
  has invested significant amounts of time and attention in the
  crop circle phenomenon. The net provides many sources of
  articles related to his work with crop circle researchers such
  as Nancy Talbott. An article authored by a Barry Chamish
  describes the findings of some such researchers and may be
  viewed at:
  http://cache.zoominfo.com/CachedPage/?ar....tName=Levengood
  The article states:
  "Several fragments of what appeared to be silicon were found at
  the center of the circle formation. a. the energy dispersive
  spectroscopy (EDS) curve confirms the fact that these crystals
  are pure silicon. b. it is interesting to note that there are
  no "growth lines" as would normally be expected if these
  crystals were thermochemically grown as a pure single crystal.
  Silicon does not normally occur in this pure form. c. it is
  highly unusual to find this type of material out in a field or
  pasture, since it is very expensive to produce." An article
  written by a David Perkins addresses the possibilities of the
  military intelligence community being involved in circumstances
  such as described above and may be viewed at:
  http://ourstrangeplanet.com/index.php?op....id=74&Itemid=69Perki
  ns writes:
  "Lately Dr. Levengood has been branching out in his research.
  According to Nancy Talbott of BLT, he recently undertook an
  unpublicized' . personal experiment' to attempt to ascertain the
  reality of the so-called' . alien abduction phenomenon.' Many
  attempts have been made in the past to monitor abductees' homes
  with video cameras, tape recorders and other gear. Invariably,


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/may/m29-003.shtml[10/12/2011 21:06:40]
Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions &

  the electronics failed at the crucial moments. A woman
  acquaintance of Dr. Levengood's approached him for help in
  determining the true nature of her night time abduction
  experiences. Dr. Levengood prepared a numbered series of gelatin
  capsules filled with wheat seeds. The woman was instructed to
  place one of the gel caps in her hair curlers each night before
  she went to bed. Dr. Levengood later collected the capsules and
  set about sprouting the seeds. All the samples sprouted normally
  except for one, which was 'dead, totally fried'. Sure enough,
  the sample coincided with the night she felt she had been
  abducted.' It was like somebody put a microwave to her head,'
  said Talbott. Not everyone is convinced that mutilations, crop
  circles and UFO events are other-wordly.
  In 1993, Phillips Laboratory, an advanced weapons research
  facility at Kirtland Air Force Base in Albuquerque, New Mexico
  issued a press-release announcing the completion of its new High
  Energy Research and Technology Facility. The release stated:

  The facility provides a unique capability for the development of
  high-power microwaves, high-energy advanced pulsed power and
  very-high- energy plasmas.' Familiar terms? The open literature
  suggests that the military started developing this new
  generation of weaponry during World War II.
  According to researcher/writer Jerry Decker:
  Since the inception of the Star Wars Program, the military has
  had the capability of projecting high intensity laser beams
  (using Eximer lasers) followed immediately by extremely high
  electrical charges aimed at targets on the planet.' We know from
  government documents that the military already possesses a wide
  range of High Power Microwave (HPM) devices with such Buck
  Rogers names as Vircators, Magnetrons, and Relativistic
  Klystrons... Sure makes you wonder." On a related note and in an
  article titled Of Flattened Flora and
  Expulsion Cavities: The crop circle controversy continues,
  author/researcher Vallee comments:
  "From the point of view of rational analysis the weight of evidence
  is still on the side of the skeptics who assure us that all crop
  circles are made by artists and lovable, jolly old men... But there
  are facts that don't quite fit, and the alternatives are worth
  considering. They lead into very disturbing areas, not all of which
  have to do with physics. In a concluding (fourth) post, I plan to
  come back to the initial issue raised by the crop circle problem,
  which is that of the construction and manipulation of belief
  systems."

  Comments, anyone?
  Thank you,
  Jack Brewer

  Note the fair use disclaimer at the bottom of the page. Not
  true.

  Don Ledger

  Smirked over an ascoted smoking jacket and a tasteful libation
  while basking in the ironically cooling glow of rank westernism,
  an overweening anthropomorphism, a too comfortable lifestyle,
  and an inflated, largely unearned social confidence. Our Mr.
  Rimmer suggests, perhaps, that it might be prudent for a
  supposed "other" to rush into a conjectured kindergarten and
  just start slapping the kids around. That they're not doing this
  is proof enough to be arrogantly cute with regard to his
  intimation that "they" cannot be "there," providing for some
  communication of a speculated variety.
  Though, there's always something outside the system, eh? Always
  an Ox to be gored. It doesn't matter how much a ridiculous
  Reductionism and over-reaching Cartesianism might _imply_ a
  comfortable deadlock nut on the truth and an improving quality
  of life. One finds that nothing is bolted together at all in a
  way insuring _certainty_ of the truly meaningful the way society
  and culture inculcates that it is. Cantor, Boltzmann, G=F6del, and
  Turing pointed this out mathematically, I understand, even if
  they couldn't handle it in the aggregate. William Shakespeare
  and our Mr. Rimmer's finest works _are_ as filthy rags on most
  levels of consideration in this multiverse. Forget that and
  eventually be humiliated, I suspect.
  There, not as funny if aspiring to be as clever.

  >>Smirked over an ascoted smoking jacket and a tasteful libation
  >>while basking in the ironically cooling glow of rank westernism,
  >>an overweening anthropomorphism, a too comfortable lifestyle,
  >>and an inflated, largely unearned social confidence.
  >It's always a delight to stimulate Herr Lehmberg to such heights
  >of rhapsodic indignation by pointing out a simple truth, i.e. no
  >matter how much caleotropic rhetoric he expends propounding the
  >contrary view, there is no evidence whatsoever that his dearly
  >longed-for aliens are in any way troubling the scorers, as we
  >say on this side of The Pond.
  >My ascoted smoking jacket is away at the dry cleaners presently,
  >and the Royal Ascot meeting is still a fortnight off (do you
  >have fortnights in the US of A?) and I hope it will be ready for
  >me to cut a fine dash on Ladies' Day.
  >Tootle-pip, old chap,
  >John 'Ironically Cool' Rimmer
  Hi, John/List
  Isn't it more for others to determine if you are actually
  "Ironically Cool" than to apply such a self-elevating term to
  yourself, or was that done with a knowingly sly wink in
  anticipation of what you must realize would be the reaction? I
  highly suspect Lehmberg's use of the phrase "ironically cooling
  glow of rank westernism" may have been intended rather
  differently than your abbreviated appropriation of it. 8^}
  Also, I don't think "caleotropic" is a genuine English word; I
  tried using dictionary.com and google, and it does not appear.
  Is it some esoterically bizarre neologism or portmanteau?
  What did you intend to mean? Is this some oddly idiomatic
  Anglicism? Or subtly abstruse slight toward the state of
  California, where I happen to live? If so, you should know
  Monsieur Lehmberg resides in southern Alabama, not Cali.
  As for "troubling the scorers" I can only assume this is some
  generically obscure reference to cricket--I had no idea that
  aliens had ever caused difficulty for those involved in this
  strange English sport, but I would not dismiss the possibility
  out of hand, as we must admit we truly do not know as yet.
  Pip-pip and cheerio,
  Steve
